Two consequences: the release gate is flaky on correct code (~5% per run), and a real user is told to close an app that is not running. Ticketed T-230; convergence returns to stage C per CONVERGE.md.
- 02.09.26 10:37 [E-763] [parent: E-762] [T-230] RUN: build -> tools/install-electron.js fsRetry(): RETRIABLE_FS_CODES = EBUSY/EPERM/EACCES, bounded backoff 25/50/100/200/400ms (775ms total, 6 attempts), sync sleep via Atomics.wait on a throwaway SharedArrayBuffer with a busy-wait fallback. Every mutating rename/copy in BOTH transactions routes through it -- in-place backup copy, retire-old-relocation, staging move, archive move, unpacked move, in-place revert restore, relocation revert restore -- AND the rollback undo steps, because a rollback that loses to an indexer leaves the app half-moved, which is worse than the original failure. Non-retriable codes rethrow immediately (retrying ENOENT would only make a missing file slow). WINTAGE_TEST_NO_FS_RETRY seam added so a gate can still measure the raw flake. The 'application is running' message is unchanged and still gated on EBUSY/EPERM: a genuinely running app holds its archive for as long as it runs, so it exhausts every attempt and still reports correctly.

Third instance of the same defect class this run: the fix already existed in the file and the risky call sites bypassed it. Git-Safe (added precisely for this hazard, with -c core.autocrlf=false AND stderr merged) is used by every fire-and-forget git call, but the NINE calls whose value is read back were written as bare `& git ... 2>$null` -- and `2>$null` does not help, because PS 5.1 under EAP=Stop promotes native stderr regardless. `git stash create` emits one warning per CRLF-converted file, so the snapshot step could never survive a dirty tree of this shape.

(1) CORE-003 P1, wintage.user.js:80-84: `window.__wintageExcludedReload` is set to true BEFORE `location.reload()`, and the catch swallows a throw without clearing it -- one refused reload disarms the OAuth/captcha/payment exclusion guard PERMANENTLY for that document, leaving the theme mutating auth UI. Same defect class as T-226 (refused reload leaves a silent split brain), which I fixed for the palette switch and missed here. My own gate test-spa-exclude.js:155-166 asserts the latch suppresses later reloads but its shim's reload always succeeds, so refusal was never covered. (2) CORE-001 P0 DATA LOSS, tools/install-electron.js:404-428 relocation Revert: reproduced in an isolated fixture with app.asar + app.asar.unpacked -- apply exit 0, revert with WINTAGE_TEST_FAIL_AFTER_REVERT exit 1, and afterwards NEITHER root app.asar NOR app/app.asar exists (unpacked left orphaned at root) while the tool prints 'restored the exact pre-operation state'. Root cause: line 416 deletes app/ before rollback, then 422 writes into the now-missing dir (swallowed) and 423 deletes the live root archive; captureAppDir at 584-590 never captures the archive, and targets.ps1:201-227 classifies themed-relocated as repaintOnly so the parent snapshot omits it too. Both fixes are ticketed, neither is applied yet.

Now finalize KEEPS the restoration snapshot and its original-path evidence, drops only the live-install markers (palette, active-path) and the consumed recovery (replaced-theme bytes, created marker), and WRITES the retired marker, making the documented epoch lifecycle executable: the next fresh Apply re-baselines from the then-current theme, overwrites the retired snapshot in place (the path Windows may still hold stays valid), and clears the marker only after the fresh snapshot is committed. Repaint inside a live epoch is untouched and still preserves the baseline byte-for-byte. W2-002, install-obs.js: one iniLookup helper now does the case-INSENSITIVE section/key match the writers already do, and every reader uses it -- currentThemeKeyValue, the first-Apply capture, and legacy INI recovery. readThemeSnapshot selects the format by an unambiguous rule (opens with {, or opens with [ only when it is genuinely an INI section header) and validates the JSON schema strictly: existed must be boolean, existed:true requires a string value, existed:false forbids one. Malformed or wrong-shape recovery is now UNREADABLE instead of quietly reinterpreted as a well-formed {existed:false,value:null} that the fail-closed preflight accepted and Revert then used to delete a live Theme key. The file's own casing is never rewritten; only lookup semantics were ever ambiguous.

- 04.09.26 02:09 [E-839] [parent: E-838] [T-234] [agent: opencode] RUN: build wave 3 (SRC-004 R005/R008/R009/R010/R011/R012) -> CORE-005: $null is now the ONLY absence sentinel in both ownership restorers. Get-IniKey has always returned '' for a present-empty `Key=` and $null for absent, and both snapshots kept all three states -- only Restore-TotalCmdOwned and the qBittorrent Revert collapsed present-empty into absent, so reverting a setting the user had as `Key=` silently changed the file's shape. W2-003, common.ps1 Get-InstallEpoch: first creation is a real race with ONE winner -- a dedicated OS lock file (install-epoch.lock, FileShare::None) held across absent-check + create + re-read, so every loser returns the PERSISTED id instead of its own private GUID. The loser used to stamp recovery provenance with an identity the file never carried, and Assert-RecoveryProvenance then rejected Wintage's own backup as foreign. Lock ordering is stated in-file (no path holds a target lock and then wants the epoch lock while another holds the epoch lock and wants a target lock), and abandoned creation temps are cleaned deterministically. W2-004: the rollback boundary moved OUT to cover preflight -> snapshot -> mutate -> commit for the generic VS Code-family extension, OBS, qBittorrent Apply AND Revert, conhost and MPC-HC. Every one of them took a byte-exact snapshot and then mutated OUTSIDE Invoke-TargetCommit, so the snapshot only ever protected the manifest write; qBittorrent Revert additionally checked its required pristine AFTER rewriting the live INI. W2-005: rollback is checked and honest -- Restore-ObsPreState verifies its own result against the snapshot bytes and throws INCOMPLETE (a failed read-back is a verification failure, not an escape), the Terminal manifest-failure restore and the MPC-HC reg import go through Invoke-Native, and a double failure now reports BOTH the original commit error and the rollback error instead of replacing the commit one. W2-006: first-touch recovery creation moved INSIDE the ShouldProcess gate, so -WhatIf is strictly zero-write; it used to be the first writer of persistent recovery state, or crash on a recovery.json write whose directory the suppressed New-Item never created. W2-007: paths.json is a serialized UPDATE for BOTH writers (one paths.lock held across read -> merge -> temp -> replace, the read taken after the lock), and Ask-CustomPath propagates a save failure and rolls the in-memory value back instead of logging "folder set" for a path that lives only in this session. One collateral defect found while wiring the OBS transaction: $args inside a scriptblock is that block's OWN empty automatic variable, so `& node $args` there would have run node with no arguments at all -- renamed $obsArgs. Two test seams added (WINTAGE_TEST_FAIL_AFTER_OBS_THEME, WINTAGE_TEST_FAIL_AFTER_EXT_COPY) plus WINTAGE_TEST_ALLOW_RUNNING_OBS, without which the OBS gate passes for the wrong reason on any machine with OBS open (the run refuses before mutating, which looks exactly like a clean rollback).

Both now ancestor-collapse in both directions, cap the queue (MAX_TREE_ROOTS/MAX_DIRTY/MAX_AD_ROOTS) and represent overflow as ONE document-wide continuation -- a strict superset of the dropped roots -- and SCROLL_FIX drains through a head index instead of Array.shift (O(n) per removal). PERF-003, the light lane: `querySelectorAll('*:not([data-w95-done])')` materialised the COMPLETE NodeList before the 2500-node budget was consulted (12,000 matches for 2,500 calls) and cost a full selector walk per root even on a settled page. The lane now drains an explicit bounded registry (lightDirty, LIGHT_DIRTY_MAX 2000, overflow promoted once to the already-incremental force lane) and the pending token is CONSUMED before the pass it caused, so one isolated request no longer costs two sweeps. PERF-004, shadow lifetime: onMutations handled no removedNodes at all, so a quiet removal scheduled nothing, and MutationObserver has no per-target unobserve -- the detached root stayed a registered target until suspendRepainter. A removal-bearing batch now schedules ONE bounded prune that scans piercedRoots (bounded by what we pierced, not by what was removed), drops disconnected roots and rebuilds the shared registration from the survivors, taking pending records first and handing them back so a legitimate shadow mutation in the same tick is not lost. SHADOW_OBS_OPTS is declared once so the rebuild cannot drift from the original registration. PERF-006, WCO_FIX: requestAnimationFrame does NOT coalesce separately queued callbacks, so each resize/geometrychange EVENT queued a full querySelectorAll + getBoundingClientRect scan (100 events = 100 scans, 100,000 rect reads, 99 superseded). One frameQueued latch, cleared immediately before apply() so the latest geometry still wins -- a latch, not a debounce, because a debounce makes controls lag the window. PERF-007, injection epoch: did-navigate-in-page bumped the document epoch, so the next did-frame-finish-load (constant in an SPA) read the SAME document as fresh and stacked another stylesheet while wc.__wintageCssKey advanced past the previous one, making it unremovable. In-page navigation no longer touches the epoch (a real reload fires did-navigate), and the previous key is retired via removeInsertedCSS BEFORE the replacement installs, with the stored key updated only after insertCSS resolves.

- 04.09.26 11:45 [E-847] [parent: E-846] [T-234] [agent: opencode] RUN: build wave 5 (SRC-004 R013) -> PERF-001, tools/install-electron.js: recovery was stacked whole-binary Buffers across BOTH transaction layers. captureRevertPreState read the live archive, its .bak, the executable and the fuse backup into RAM; captureAppDir then read the MOVED archive and the complete app.asar.unpacked tree into more Buffers, so the same archive was resident TWICE (pre.movedAsar AND appPre['app.asar']); installInPlace held a full pre-patch Buffer in ADDITION to the .bak copy it had just written one line earlier; and the parent PowerShell transaction was independently copying all of it to disk anyway. Measured by the audit at +192.1 MiB RSS for a 64 MiB archive plus a 64 MiB unpacked file -- roughly one byte of RSS per recovery byte, at exactly the moment memory pressure is least welcome. Recovery is now a durable on-disk VAULT plus in-memory IDENTITY: vaultStore copies a large file once into an OS-temp vault, and the snapshot carries presence, kind, size and a streamed SHA-256 (64 KiB window) instead of bytes. sameSnapshot compares size+digest rather than Buffer.equals, so appDirByteIdentical no longer materialises a whole tree to answer one boolean, and describePath answers the same question without copying at all. Three consequences beyond the memory, and they are the reason this shape was chosen: the vault is DURABLE, so recovery evidence survives an incomplete rollback instead of dying with the process, and it is named as a recovery location in every INCOMPLETE message; the relocation-revert rollback now restores the app dir FIRST, because that snapshot owns the moved archive's bytes and the root copy may only be dropped once the moved one is back; and the in-place rollback reads the on-disk .bak, which is digest-verified against the live archive BEFORE the first write, so an unverified copy can never become the rollback authority. Vault lifetime is deliberate: dropped on exit for a clean run, retained ONLY when the rollback could not put everything back, because then it holds bytes nobody else does and deleting it would turn a recoverable failure into data loss. A rename-only stock->relocation apply copies nothing and therefore creates no vault at all.
